## Wave 1

**37-01: SDK Batch Upload Pipeline**
New `uploadFiles()` method on `CipherBoxClient` that encrypts and pins N files in parallel (max 3 concurrent via p-limit), collects results, re-reads folder metadata to avoid stale-children races, adds all successful FilePointers, and does a single folder IPNS publish. This is the foundation that Wave 2's UI layer calls into.

## Wave 2

**37-02: Web Worker Encryption + useDropUpload Rewire**
Creates a dedicated Web Worker (`encrypt.worker.ts`) that moves AES-256-GCM file encryption off the main thread using Transferable ArrayBuffer for zero-copy data passing. A service wrapper (`encrypt-worker.service.ts`) provides a Promise-based API. Then `useDropUpload` is rewired to call the SDK's `uploadFiles()` batch method with the Worker-backed `encryptFn`, replacing the current sequential upload loop.

# Roadmap: CipherBox v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure

## Overview

CipherBox v1.1 transforms the platform from "IPFS as a storage backend with database fallbacks" to "IPFS-native with the database serving only auth." The milestone establishes performance baselines before making changes, replaces the unreliable delegated-ipfs.dev dependency with self-hosted IPNS resolution, migrates rootFolderKey to an IPFS vault blob (achieving true zero-knowledge server), adds BYO-IPFS node support for data sovereignty, and completes performance baselines with client-side instrumentation and load testing after all features are stable.

## Milestones

- **v0.1 Staging MVP** - Phases 1-10 (shipped 2026-02-11)
- **v1.0 Production** - Phases 11-17.1 (shipped 2026-03-05)
- **v1.1 IPFS Infrastructure** - Phases 18-22 (in progress)

## Phases

**Phase Numbering:**

- Integer phases (1, 2, 3): Planned milestone work
- Decimal phases (2.1, 2.2): Urgent insertions (marked with INSERTED)

Decimal phases appear between their surrounding integers in numeric order.

- [x] **Phase 18: Performance Instrumentation** - Server-side Prometheus histograms and Kubo metrics scraping to establish baselines before any architectural changes (completed 2026-03-07)
- [x] **Phase 19: IPNS Resolution Improvement** - Replace delegated-ipfs.dev with self-hosted Someguy sidecar for reliable IPNS routing, add latency histograms for resolve/publish operations (completed 2026-03-07)
- [x] **Phase 19.2: IPFS Upload Performance Optimization** - Optimize Kubo pinning path (concurrent pins, worker tuning, pin batching) to reduce upload latency (INSERTED) (completed 2026-03-23)
- [x] **Phase 20: Vault Migration** - Move rootFolderKey to IPFS vault blob v2 format, making the server store zero crypto material (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-24)
- [x] **Phase 21: BYO-IPFS Node Support** - User-configurable IPFS pinning endpoint with dual-pin strategy, Settings UI, and connection testing (gap closure in progress) (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 22: Performance Baselines Completion** - Client-side timing instrumentation, end-to-end journey timing, Vitest-based load testing, and capacity documentation (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 23: Rust SDK Extraction** - Extract five Rust crates (crypto, core, api-client, fuse, sdk) mirroring the TypeScript SDK hierarchy, replace duplicated logic in desktop FUSE code, enable unit testing at same granularity as TypeScript (completed 2026-03-24)
- [x] **Phase 24: Bug Fixes & Test Infrastructure** - Fix known bugs (bin IPNS 404, device registry format error) and strengthen test infrastructure (headless load tests, vault recovery E2E, load test auth refresh) (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 25: Desktop Enhancements** - Desktop auto-update mechanism and TEE file enrollment for new files (completed 2026-03-25)
- [x] **Phase 26: Observability & UX Tuning** - Grafana alerting thresholds from existing baselines and timeout tuning for sub-2s UX (completed 2026-03-26)
- [x] **Phase 28: Code Hygiene & Logging** - Structured logger wrapper, replace 124 console.\* calls, fix silenced unpin failures, clean any casts, archive legacy POC (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 29: Infrastructure Hardening** - Wire up IPNS unenrollment on deletion, test login endpoint hardening, IPFS node access control (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 30: Web App Observability** - Error tracking service, error boundaries, client-side telemetry (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 31: Structural Decomposition** - Split monolithic files (useSharedNavigation, FileBrowser, folder.service) into focused modules (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 32: FUSE Async FilePointer Resolution** - Channel-based async resolution to prevent Finder disconnects from blocking FUSE thread (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 33: Windows Async FilePointer Resolution** - Port Phase 32's channel-based async FilePointer resolution to the WinFsp backend (completed 2026-03-28)
- [x] **Phase 34: E2E Test Expansion & Staging Baselines** - Streaming playback, media preview, batch download, and shared teardown E2E tests; BYO-IPFS load test and Faro metrics baselines on staging (completed 2026-03-29)

### Gaps Found

4 gaps blocking goal achievement — all from the same root cause (Plan 02 not executed):

1. **File encryption runs in a Web Worker thread** — `apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts` does not exist. `apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts` does not exist.

2. **Main thread stays responsive during batch uploads** — Encryption still runs on the main thread via the sequential `uploadFile()` loop. No Web Worker implemented.

3. **useDropUpload calls client.uploadFiles() for new files** — `apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` lines 113-133 still uses a sequential `for` loop calling `client.uploadFile()` per file (N IPNS publishes for N files). Zero references to `client.uploadFiles` anywhere in `apps/web/src/`.

4. **Worker is terminated on SDK client destroy/logout** — `destroyEncryptionWorker()` never referenced in `apps/web/src/lib/sdk-provider.ts`.

**Plan 01 is fully verified:** `uploadFiles()` method exists (line 840 of `packages/sdk/src/client.ts`), is wired with p-limit concurrency pool, stale-children re-read via `loadFolderMetadata()`, single `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish()` call, and all 10 unit tests pass in the worktree. The SDK capability is ready — it just is not connected to the web app.

**Additional finding:** The `p-limit@7.3.0` package is in `pnpm-lock.yaml` but `pnpm install` has not been run on the main repo tree, so tests fail there. This is an infrastructure issue (normal for worktree-based development) — not a code defect. All 148 unit tests pass in the worktree.

Structured gaps in VERIFICATION.md frontmatter for `/gsd:plan-phase --gaps`.

## Phase 37: Parallel Batch Upload Pipeline — Complete

**Waves:** 2 | **Plans:** 2/2 complete | **Verification:** Passed (12/12 truths, 12/12 requirements)

| Wave | Plans | Status |
|------|-------|--------|
| 1 | 37-01 | ✓ Complete |
| 2 | 37-02 | ✓ Complete |

### Plan Details

1. **37-01**: SDK `uploadFiles()` — p-limit(3) concurrency pool, stale-metadata re-read, single IPNS publish, `files:batchUploaded` event, 10 unit tests
2. **37-02**: Web Worker encryption offload (`encrypt.worker.ts` + `EncryptionWorkerService`) + `useDropUpload` rewired to batch API, worker cleanup on logout

### Issues Encountered

None

159	diff --git a/apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts b/apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts
160	new file mode 100644
161	index 000000000..f62f5d4af
162	--- /dev/null
163	+++ b/apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts
164	@@ -0,0 +1,147 @@
165	+import type { ExternalEncryptFn } from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
166	+import type { EncryptRequest, EncryptResponse } from '../workers/encrypt.worker';
167	+
168	+/**
169	+ * Main-thread wrapper for the encryption Web Worker.
170	+ *
171	+ * Creates one Worker instance lazily on first use and reuses it for all
172	+ * encryption operations. Each encrypt() call sends a message with a
173	+ * correlation ID and returns a Promise resolved by the matching response.
174	+ *
175	+ * Uses Transferable ArrayBuffer transfers in both directions to avoid
176	+ * copying large file data between threads.
177	+ */
178	+export class EncryptionWorkerService {
179	+  private worker: Worker | null = null;
180	+  private pending = new Map<
181	+    string,
182	+    {
183	+      resolve: (value: {
184	+        ciphertext: Uint8Array;
185	+        wrappedKey: string;
186	+        iv: string;
187	+        fileKey: Uint8Array;
188	+        originalSize: number;
189	+        encryptedSize: number;
190	+      }) => void;
191	+      reject: (reason: Error) => void;
192	+    }
193	+  >();
194	+  private idCounter = 0;
195	+
196	+  /** Lazily create the Worker on first use. */
197	+  private getWorker(): Worker {
198	+    if (!this.worker) {
199	+      // Static string literal required for Vite static analysis
200	+      this.worker = new Worker(
201	+        new URL('../workers/encrypt.worker.ts', import.meta.url),
202	+        { type: 'module' },
203	+      );
204	+      this.worker.onmessage = (event: MessageEvent<EncryptResponse>) => {
205	+        const response = event.data;
206	+        const entry = this.pending.get(response.id);
207	+        if (!entry) return;
208	+        this.pending.delete(response.id);
209	+
210	+        if (response.type === 'error') {
211	+          entry.reject(new Error(response.error));
212	+        } else {
213	+          entry.resolve({
214	+            ciphertext: response.ciphertext,
215	+            wrappedKey: response.wrappedKey,
216	+            iv: response.iv,
217	+            fileKey: response.fileKey,
218	+            originalSize: response.originalSize,
219	+            encryptedSize: response.encryptedSize,
220	+          });
221	+        }
222	+      };
223	+      this.worker.onerror = (event) => {
224	+        // Reject all pending operations on worker crash
225	+        for (const [, entry] of this.pending) {
226	+          entry.reject(new Error(`Worker error: ${event.message}`));
227	+        }
228	+        this.pending.clear();
229	+      };
230	+    }
231	+    return this.worker;
232	+  }
233	+
234	+  /**
235	+   * Encrypt file data in the Web Worker.
236	+   *
237	+   * Transfers the data ArrayBuffer to the Worker (zero-copy) and receives
238	+   * the ciphertext ArrayBuffer back (zero-copy). After calling this, the
239	+   * original `data` Uint8Array becomes zero-length (transferred).
240	+   */
241	+  encrypt(params: {
242	+    data: Uint8Array;
243	+    userPublicKey: Uint8Array;
244	+    encryptionMode: 'GCM' | 'CTR';
245	+  }): Promise<{
246	+    ciphertext: Uint8Array;
247	+    wrappedKey: string;
248	+    iv: string;
249	+    fileKey: Uint8Array;
250	+    originalSize: number;
251	+    encryptedSize: number;
252	+  }> {
253	+    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
254	+      this.idCounter += 1;
255	+      const id = `enc-${this.idCounter}-${Date.now()}`;
256	+      this.pending.set(id, { resolve, reject });
257	+
258	+      const worker = this.getWorker();
259	+      const message: EncryptRequest = {
260	+        type: 'encrypt',
261	+        id,
262	+        data: params.data,
263	+        userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
264	+        encryptionMode: params.encryptionMode,
265	+      };
266	+
267	+      // Transfer the data buffer to worker (zero-copy, original becomes empty)
268	+      worker.postMessage(message, [params.data.buffer] as Transferable[]);
269	+    });
270	+  }
271	+
272	+  /**
273	+   * Build an ExternalEncryptFn compatible with sdkCore.uploadFile's encryptFn param.
274	+   * This is the function passed to client.uploadFiles({ encryptFn }).
275	+   */
276	+  createEncryptFn(): ExternalEncryptFn {
277	+    return (params) => this.encrypt(params);
278	+  }
279	+
280	+  /**
281	+   * Terminate the Worker and reject all pending operations.
282	+   * Call on logout or SDK client destroy.
283	+   */
284	+  destroy(): void {
285	+    if (this.worker) {
286	+      this.worker.terminate();
287	+      this.worker = null;
288	+    }
289	+    for (const [, entry] of this.pending) {
290	+      entry.reject(new Error('EncryptionWorkerService destroyed'));
291	+    }
292	+    this.pending.clear();
293	+  }
294	+}
295	+
296	+/** Singleton instance. Created lazily, destroyed on logout. */
297	+let instance: EncryptionWorkerService | null = null;
298	+
299	+export function getEncryptionWorker(): EncryptionWorkerService {
300	+  if (!instance) {
301	+    instance = new EncryptionWorkerService();
302	+  }
303	+  return instance;
304	+}
305	+
306	+export function destroyEncryptionWorker(): void {
307	+  if (instance) {
308	+    instance.destroy();
309	+    instance = null;
310	+  }
311	+}

316	+++ b/apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts
317	@@ -0,0 +1,80 @@
318	+/// <reference lib="webworker" />
319	+
320	+import {
321	+  generateFileKey,
322	+  generateIv,
323	+  generateCtrIv,
324	+  encryptAesGcm,
325	+  encryptAesCtr,
326	+  wrapKey,
327	+  clearBytes,
328	+  bytesToHex,
329	+} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
330	+
331	+export type EncryptRequest = {
332	+  type: 'encrypt';
333	+  id: string;
334	+  data: Uint8Array;
335	+  userPublicKey: Uint8Array;
336	+  encryptionMode: 'GCM' | 'CTR';
337	+};
338	+
339	+export type EncryptResponse =
340	+  | {
341	+      type: 'success';
342	+      id: string;
343	+      ciphertext: Uint8Array;
344	+      wrappedKey: string;
345	+      iv: string;
346	+      fileKey: Uint8Array;
347	+      originalSize: number;
348	+      encryptedSize: number;
349	+    }
350	+  | {
351	+      type: 'error';
352	+      id: string;
353	+      error: string;
354	+    };
355	+
356	+self.onmessage = async (event: MessageEvent<EncryptRequest>) => {
357	+  const { id, data, userPublicKey, encryptionMode } = event.data;
358	+  try {
359	+    const fileKey = generateFileKey();
360	+    const iv = encryptionMode === 'CTR' ? generateCtrIv() : generateIv();
361	+
362	+    const ciphertext =
363	+      encryptionMode === 'CTR'
364	+        ? await encryptAesCtr(data, fileKey, iv)
365	+        : await encryptAesGcm(data, fileKey, iv);
366	+
367	+    const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, userPublicKey);
368	+
369	+    // Defensive copy of fileKey before clearing internal one
370	+    const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);
371	+    clearBytes(fileKey);
372	+
373	+    const response: EncryptResponse = {
374	+      type: 'success',
375	+      id,
376	+      ciphertext,
377	+      wrappedKey: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),
378	+      iv: bytesToHex(iv),
379	+      fileKey: fileKeyCopy,
380	+      originalSize: data.byteLength,
381	+      encryptedSize: ciphertext.byteLength,
382	+    };
383	+
384	+    // Transfer ownership of large buffers (zero-copy)
385	+    self.postMessage(response, [
386	+      ciphertext.buffer,
387	+      fileKeyCopy.buffer,
388	+    ] as Transferable[]);
389	+  } catch (err) {
390	+    const response: EncryptResponse = {
391	+      type: 'error',
392	+      id,
393	+      error: (err as Error).message,
394	+    };
395	+    self.postMessage(response);
396	+  }
397	+};

Excellent. Now I have a complete picture. Let me generate the final report:

## Phase 37 Code Reuse Review - Findings

I've analyzed the Phase 37 changes for code reuse issues. Here are the findings:

### **1. ISSUE: EncryptResponse type duplicated across boundaries (HIGH PRIORITY)**

- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts` (lines 22-37)
- **Also defined in:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts` (lines 31-42, called `ExternalEncryptFn` return type)
- **Finding:** The encryption result structure (ciphertext, wrappedKey, iv, fileKey, originalSize, encryptedSize) is defined in two places:
  - Worker response type: `EncryptResponse.success` (lines 22-32)
  - SDK contract: `ExternalEncryptFn` return type (lines 35-42)
  - Web service wrapper: `EncryptionWorkerService.encrypt()` return type (lines 81-88 in encrypt-worker.service.ts)
  - Web app legacy: `EncryptedFileResult` in file-crypto.service.ts (lines 12-19)

**Recommendation:** Extract a shared encryption result type to a common types module or export it from `@cipherbox/sdk-core` so all layers use the same contract.

---

### **2. ISSUE: Encryption logic duplicated between worker and sdk-core (HIGH PRIORITY)**

- **Worker implementation:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts` (lines 42-54)
  - Generates fileKey, IV
  - Encrypts with GCM or CTR
  - Wraps key with ECIES
  - Clears sensitive bytes

- **SDK-core implementation:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts` (lines 134-147)
  - Identical logic in the "internal encryption path"
  - Only difference: CTR mode uses different IV generator (`generateCtrIv` vs `generateIv`)

**Finding:** Both implementations are mirror code that could be collapsed. The worker shouldn't duplicate the core algorithm.

**Recommendation:** Move encryption logic to a shared utility module (e.g., `packages/sdk-core/src/upload/encrypt-core.ts`). The worker would import and call it, eliminating duplication.

---

### **3. ISSUE: Multiple definitions of the same result shape**

- **apps/web/src/services/file-crypto.service.ts** line 12: `EncryptedFileResult`
- **apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts** line 22: `EncryptResponse`  
- **packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts** line 31: `ExternalEncryptFn` return type (anonymous)
- **apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts** line 81: `encrypt()` return type (anonymous)

All have identical fields. This creates maintenance burden if the encryption result structure changes.

---

### **4. ISSUE: Upfront file.arrayBuffer() reading pattern (MODERATE)**

- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` (lines 126-139)
- **Pattern:** Reads all files into memory with `await file.arrayBuffer()` before passing to SDK batch operation
- **Finding:** This loads entire file contents upfront (lines 126-139 loop), even if some files might be cancelled before encryption starts
- **Context:** The old `streaming-crypto.service.ts` (line 78) uses lazy chunk-based reading (`file.slice()`) to keep memory bounded
- **Impact:** For multi-GB files in batch uploads, this could cause memory pressure before the worker gets to process them

**Recommendation:** Consider lazy reading — pass File objects directly to SDK's `uploadFiles()` and let the worker/upload path read chunks on-demand. Check whether the SDK contract can accept File objects instead of requiring Uint8Array upfront (current design forces eager reads).

---

### **5. ISSUE: Inline validation logic in useDropUpload**

- **File:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` (lines 40-88)
- **Duplicate validation blocks:**
  - Lines 40-45: MAX_FILE_SIZE check
  - Lines 50-55: Quota validation
  - Lines 57-65: Batch name collision check
  - Lines 67-88: Folder existence and name collision checks

**Finding:** This validation logic is tightly coupled to the hook. If the same validations are needed elsewhere (e.g., drag-drop on different components, or future folder upload), the logic would need to be extracted.

**Recommendation:** Extract to a `validateFileBatch()` utility function in a shared service module.

---

### **6. No critical issue: Encryption mode selection**

- **Pattern appears in:** 
  - `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/services/streaming-crypto.service.ts` (line 36)
  - `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts` (line 108)
- **Finding:** Both delegate to `selectEncryptionMode()` from sdk-core, so NO duplication here. This is handled correctly.

---

### Summary Table

| Issue | Severity | Files | Root Cause |
|-------|----------|-------|-----------|
| EncryptResponse type defined 4 times | High | worker, sdk-core, service, legacy | No shared type export |
| Encryption algorithm duplicated | High | worker ↔ sdk-core | Worker has own impl of core logic |
| Eager file.arrayBuffer() reads | Moderate | useDropUpload | SDK contract forces Uint8Array |
| Validation logic scattered | Low | useDropUpload | Inline instead of extracted utility |

No issues found with:
- Worker lifecycle management (well-encapsulated in EncryptionWorkerService)
- Transferable buffer usage (properly implemented)
- Key cleanup (both paths clear sensitive bytes)
- Encryption mode selection (correctly centralized)

Perfect. Now I have a complete picture. Let me compile my findings into a comprehensive report.

## Phase 37 Efficiency Review Report

Based on my thorough analysis of all Phase 37 changed files, I've identified **one significant efficiency concern and several minor observations**:

### CRITICAL EFFICIENCY ISSUE

**1. Bulk file buffer allocation before concurrency pool in useDropUpload.ts**

**File:** `apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` (lines 125–139)

**Issue:** All new files are read into `Uint8Array` buffers **sequentially and sequentially before** the p-limit(3) concurrency pool in the SDK even processes them:

```typescript
// Lines 126–139: All files read synchronously into memory FIRST
const fileEntries: Array<{ data: Uint8Array; fileName: string; mimeType: string }> = [];
for (const file of newFiles) {
  const uploadId = uploadIdMap.get(file.name)!;
  if (!useUploadStore.getState().files.get(uploadId)) {
    continue; // User cancelled this file before it started
  }
  const data = new Uint8Array(await file.arrayBuffer());  // <-- Each iteration awaits
  fileEntries.push({
    data,
    fileName: file.name,
    mimeType: file.type || 'application/octet-stream',
  });
}

// Only after ALL files are read does uploadFiles() get called
const result = await client.uploadFiles(
  parentFolder.ipnsName,
  fileEntries,
  // ...
);
```

**Impact:** For 10 files × 100MB each:
- Total memory allocated before SDK processing: ~1GB of `Uint8Array` buffers
- All buffers live in memory until `Promise.allSettled()` completes (line 889 in client.ts)
- The p-limit(3) concurrency pool throttles encrypt+pin operations, but cannot free the original buffers until all files finish

**Why this matters:**
- **Missed lazy-loading opportunity:** The SDK's uploadFiles() uses a p-limit(3) pool (good). But the caller allocates all 10 buffers upfront (bad).
- **No pipelining:** Read→encrypt→pin→free should happen per-file. Instead it's: read-all→encrypt-all→pin-all.
- **Memory spike on large batches:** The initial array buffer read loop is not bounded by the concurrency limit.

---

### DESIGN PATTERN NOTES (Not Issues)

**2. Transferable ArrayBuffer transfer is correctly implemented**

**Files:** 
- `apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts` (lines 104)
- `apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts` (lines 68–71)

**Observation:** Zero-copy transfer via `postMessage(message, [params.data.buffer])` is correct. The original `Uint8Array` in `useDropUpload` is already allocated *before* transfer, so the Transferable optimization reduces overhead on the round-trip, but does not address the upstream bulk-read issue.

---

**3. File key cleanup is correct**

**File:** `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (lines 1055–1060)

**Observation:** The finally-block correctly clears file keys after batch publish:
```typescript
finally {
  for (const success of successes) {
    clearBytes(success.uploadResult.fileKey);
  }
}
```

This prevents key material leaking across uploads. No issue here.

---

**4. Promise.allSettled holds result objects until resolution**

**File:** `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (lines 889–913)

**Observation:** The settled array stores all FileResult objects (including uploadResult with ciphertext buffers) until the entire batch completes. However:
- Ciphertext buffers are transferred to the worker (neutered), so this is not a memory leak
- fileKey copies are cleared in finally block
- The pattern is acceptable for batch size ≤ 10

---

### MINOR OBSERVATIONS

**5. Duplicate file path reads sequentially**

**File:** `apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` (lines 195–220)

Duplicate files are encrypted via the old service sequentially (old behavior, not Phase 37). This is intentional (not registered in folder) and is fine for the expected small replacement queue.

---

**6. Double copyBytes on fileKey in encrypt.worker.ts**

**File:** `apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts` (lines 52–54)

```typescript
const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);  // Defensive copy before clear
clearBytes(fileKey);  // Clear internal copy
```

This is a defensive pattern (correct) but adds one extra allocation. The comment explains it's intentional.

---

### SUMMARY

**Real efficiency concern:** Bulk pre-allocation of all file buffers before the concurrency pool throttles. For typical use (3–5 files), impact is negligible. For power users uploading 50+ files at once, this could create memory spikes.

**No critical bugs found.** Key cleanup, Transferable transfers, and concurrency pooling are all correct.

## Phase 37 Code Quality Review

### ISSUES FOUND

#### 1. **REDUNDANT ENCRYPTION RESULT SHAPE** (High Priority)
   - **File**: `packages/sdk-core/src/upload/index.ts` (lines 31-42)
   - **Issue**: `ExternalEncryptFn` return type is defined inline as a 6-property object shape (ciphertext, wrappedKey, iv, fileKey, originalSize, encryptedSize)
   - **Also defined at**:
     - `apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts` (lines 19-26) - inline in Promise resolve type
     - `apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts` (lines 23-31) - inline in EncryptResponse success variant
     - `apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts` (lines 81-88) - inline in encrypt() return type
   - **Impact**: 4 separate type definitions of the same shape. Any future changes to encryption result structure require updating all 4 locations.
   - **Recommendation**: Create a single shared type (e.g., `EncryptionResult`) and reuse across all locations.

#### 2. **BLOB CONSTRUCTION WITH .buffer IN LEGACY PATH** (Data Corruption Risk)
   - **File**: `apps/web/src/services/upload.service.ts` (line 62)
   - **Issue**: `new Blob([encrypted.ciphertext.buffer as ArrayBuffer])` passes the entire underlying ArrayBuffer instead of the typed array itself
   - **Codebase Rule Violation**: Violates `apps/web/CLAUDE.md` (line 137-141) which explicitly states "never use .buffer" due to potential extra bytes being included
   - **Context**: This is the old single-file upload path for duplicate files (legacy service), but it's still active and dangerous
   - **Impact**: Silent data corruption risk if ArrayBuffer is larger than the view
   - **Correct Pattern**: Shown correctly in `useDropUpload.ts` (line 210): `new Blob([new Uint8Array(encrypted.ciphertext)])` - pass typed array directly

#### 3. **QUESTIONABLE DEFENSIVE COPY IN WORKER** (Architectural Question)
   - **File**: `apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts` (lines 52-54)
   - **Code**: 
     ```typescript
     const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);
     clearBytes(fileKey);
     ```
   - **Issue**: Comment says "Defensive copy of fileKey before clearing internal one," but immediately after, the worker transfers both `fileKeyCopy.buffer` and `ciphertext.buffer` via `postMessage()` (lines 68-71)
   - **Problem**: The transfer itself zeroes the buffers in the Worker context. The defensive copy is unnecessary because:
     1. `clearBytes()` only affects the Worker's copy of fileKey
     2. The transferred buffer becomes inaccessible in the Worker anyway
     3. The main thread receives a transferred buffer, not a view into the Worker's memory
   - **Recommendation**: Either remove the defensive copy (since transfer handles cleanup), or add a clearer comment explaining why it's needed for security semantics (if that's the intent)

#### 4. **PARAMETER SPRAWL IN uploadFiles()** (Moderate Issue)
   - **File**: `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (lines 840-859)
   - **Issue**: `uploadFiles()` takes an `options?` object with two separate override functions (`encryptFn` and `pinFn`), plus parallel `callbacks` object with three separate per-file handlers
   - **Pattern Sprawl**: 5 optional callback/override parameters scattered across two objects, making the API surface large
   - **Related**: `uploadFile()` has similar but slightly different parameter structure, creating cognitive load
   - **Recommendation**: Consider grouping callbacks more logically or using a builder pattern for complex initialization

#### 5. **TASK-REFERENCE COMMENTS (Code Hygiene)**
   - **File**: `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (multiple locations)
     - Line 34: `(D-01)` - comment on UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY constant
     - Lines 835-838: `(D-03, D-01, D-02, D-05, D-09, D-10)` - enumeration of requirements
     - Lines 888, 932, 944, 965: Inline `(D-02, D-10)` style references
   - **File**: `apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts`
     - Lines 26-27: `(D-03, D-07)` 
     - Line 115: `(D-03)` 
     - Line 142: `(D-07)` 
     - Line 173: `(D-11)` 
   - **Issue**: These are task requirement references from planning documents (D = design requirement), not WHY comments. They help during development but provide zero value to future maintainers reading the code.
   - **Impact**: Code noise that clutters understanding of the actual business logic
   - **Recommendation**: Remove D-0X references or replace with actual WHY context. For example, instead of "(D-03: single folder publish)", explain "single folder publish reduces IPNS conflicts in concurrent batch uploads"

#### 6. **MEMORY BUFFERING IN useDropUpload** (Design Concern)
   - **File**: `apps/web/src/hooks/useDropUpload.ts` (lines 125-139)
   - **Issue**: All file buffers are read into memory upfront with `file.arrayBuffer()` before being passed to `uploadFiles()`:
     ```typescript
     for (const file of newFiles) {
       const data = new Uint8Array(await file.arrayBuffer());
       fileEntries.push({ data, ... });
     }
     ```
   - **Problem**: For a batch of large files (e.g., 5 × 50MB files = 250MB), this buffers all 250MB simultaneously in memory before encryption/upload begins
   - **Mitigation**: The pipeline-style encryption (D-02 requirement) is supposed to process files one-at-a-time via p-limit(3), but this upfront read defeats that goal
   - **Impact**: High memory overhead for large file batches; potential OOM on memory-constrained devices
   - **Note**: This is a trade-off between early error detection (detect unreadable files before entering SDK) vs. memory efficiency. Currently prioritizes early detection. Comment (line 125) acknowledges this choice.

#### 7. **UNNECESSARY TYPE CAST IN BATCH TEST** (Minor)
   - **File**: `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/upload-batch.test.ts` (line 9)
   - **Issue**: Import type `{ UploadResult }` explicitly from SDK-core, but file already imports from `@cipherbox/sdk-core` at line 56
   - **Impact**: Minimal; improves consistency but not critical

---

### POSITIVE PATTERNS OBSERVED

- Encryption Worker correctly uses `Transferable` buffer transfers for zero-copy operations
- Proper key cleanup with `clearBytes()` in both Worker and SDK client
- Defensive copying of keypairs in CipherBoxClient constructor (lines 60-68) is correct
- Good separation of concerns: Worker handles crypto, service wraps Worker, SDK client orchestrates
- Comments on folder registration (sdk-provider.ts) explain WHY correctly
- Test helpers properly mock crypto functions

---

### SUMMARY

**Critical**: 
- Fix Blob construction in `upload.service.ts` line 62 (.buffer issue)
- Extract shared EncryptionResult type

**Important**:
- Remove/replace task-reference comments (D-0X, D-1X)
- Clarify or remove defensive copy comment in worker

**Consider**:
- Monitor memory usage with large batch uploads (current design is acceptable but documented trade-off)
- Evaluate parameter sprawl in uploadFiles callback/option structure
```

1	import type { ExternalEncryptFn } from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
2	import type { EncryptRequest, EncryptResponse } from '../workers/encrypt.worker';
3	
4	/**
5	 * Main-thread wrapper for the encryption Web Worker.
6	 *
7	 * Creates one Worker instance lazily on first use and reuses it for all
8	 * encryption operations. Each encrypt() call sends a message with a
9	 * correlation ID and returns a Promise resolved by the matching response.
10	 *
11	 * Uses Transferable ArrayBuffer transfers in both directions to avoid
12	 * copying large file data between threads.
13	 */
14	export class EncryptionWorkerService {
15	  private worker: Worker | null = null;
16	  private pending = new Map<
17	    string,
18	    {
19	      resolve: (value: {
20	        ciphertext: Uint8Array;
21	        wrappedKey: string;
22	        iv: string;
23	        fileKey: Uint8Array;
24	        originalSize: number;
25	        encryptedSize: number;
26	      }) => void;
27	      reject: (reason: Error) => void;
28	    }
29	  >();
30	  private idCounter = 0;
31	
32	  /** Lazily create the Worker on first use. */
33	  private getWorker(): Worker {
34	    if (!this.worker) {
35	      // Static string literal required for Vite static analysis
36	      this.worker = new Worker(
37	        new URL('../workers/encrypt.worker.ts', import.meta.url),
38	        { type: 'module' },
39	      );
40	      this.worker.onmessage = (event: MessageEvent<EncryptResponse>) => {
41	        const response = event.data;
42	        const entry = this.pending.get(response.id);
43	        if (!entry) return;
44	        this.pending.delete(response.id);
45	
46	        if (response.type === 'error') {
47	          entry.reject(new Error(response.error));
48	        } else {
49	          entry.resolve({
50	            ciphertext: response.ciphertext,
51	            wrappedKey: response.wrappedKey,
52	            iv: response.iv,
53	            fileKey: response.fileKey,
54	            originalSize: response.originalSize,
55	            encryptedSize: response.encryptedSize,
56	          });
57	        }
58	      };
59	      this.worker.onerror = (event) => {
60	        // Reject all pending operations on worker crash
61	        for (const [, entry] of this.pending) {
62	          entry.reject(new Error(`Worker error: ${event.message}`));
63	        }
64	        this.pending.clear();
65	      };
66	    }
67	    return this.worker;
68	  }
69	
70	  /**
71	   * Encrypt file data in the Web Worker.
72	   *
73	   * Transfers the data ArrayBuffer to the Worker (zero-copy) and receives
74	   * the ciphertext ArrayBuffer back (zero-copy). After calling this, the
75	   * original `data` Uint8Array becomes zero-length (transferred).
76	   */
77	  encrypt(params: {
78	    data: Uint8Array;
79	    userPublicKey: Uint8Array;
80	    encryptionMode: 'GCM' | 'CTR';
81	  }): Promise<{
82	    ciphertext: Uint8Array;
83	    wrappedKey: string;
84	    iv: string;
85	    fileKey: Uint8Array;
86	    originalSize: number;
87	    encryptedSize: number;
88	  }> {
89	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
90	      this.idCounter += 1;
91	      const id = `enc-${this.idCounter}-${Date.now()}`;
92	      this.pending.set(id, { resolve, reject });
93	
94	      const worker = this.getWorker();
95	      const message: EncryptRequest = {
96	        type: 'encrypt',
97	        id,
98	        data: params.data,
99	        userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
100	        encryptionMode: params.encryptionMode,
101	      };
102	
103	      // Transfer the data buffer to worker (zero-copy, original becomes empty)
104	      worker.postMessage(message, [params.data.buffer] as Transferable[]);
105	    });
106	  }
107	
108	  /**
109	   * Build an ExternalEncryptFn compatible with sdkCore.uploadFile's encryptFn param.
110	   * This is the function passed to client.uploadFiles({ encryptFn }).
111	   */
112	  createEncryptFn(): ExternalEncryptFn {
113	    return (params) => this.encrypt(params);
114	  }
115	
116	  /**
117	   * Terminate the Worker and reject all pending operations.
118	   * Call on logout or SDK client destroy.
119	   */
120	  destroy(): void {
121	    if (this.worker) {
122	      this.worker.terminate();
123	      this.worker = null;
124	    }
125	    for (const [, entry] of this.pending) {
126	      entry.reject(new Error('EncryptionWorkerService destroyed'));
127	    }
128	    this.pending.clear();
129	  }
130	}
131	
132	/** Singleton instance. Created lazily, destroyed on logout. */
133	let instance: EncryptionWorkerService | null = null;
134	
135	export function getEncryptionWorker(): EncryptionWorkerService {
136	  if (!instance) {
137	    instance = new EncryptionWorkerService();
138	  }
139	  return instance;
140	}
141	
142	export function destroyEncryptionWorker(): void {
143	  if (instance) {
144	    instance.destroy();
145	    instance = null;
146	  }
147	}
148

1	/// <reference lib="webworker" />
2	
3	import {
4	  generateFileKey,
5	  generateIv,
6	  generateCtrIv,
7	  encryptAesGcm,
8	  encryptAesCtr,
9	  wrapKey,
10	  clearBytes,
11	  bytesToHex,
12	} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
13	
14	export type EncryptRequest = {
15	  type: 'encrypt';
16	  id: string;
17	  data: Uint8Array;
18	  userPublicKey: Uint8Array;
19	  encryptionMode: 'GCM' | 'CTR';
20	};
21	
22	export type EncryptResponse =
23	  | {
24	      type: 'success';
25	      id: string;
26	      ciphertext: Uint8Array;
27	      wrappedKey: string;
28	      iv: string;
29	      fileKey: Uint8Array;
30	      originalSize: number;
31	      encryptedSize: number;
32	    }
33	  | {
34	      type: 'error';
35	      id: string;
36	      error: string;
37	    };
38	
39	self.onmessage = async (event: MessageEvent<EncryptRequest>) => {
40	  const { id, data, userPublicKey, encryptionMode } = event.data;
41	  try {
42	    const fileKey = generateFileKey();
43	    const iv = encryptionMode === 'CTR' ? generateCtrIv() : generateIv();
44	
45	    const ciphertext =
46	      encryptionMode === 'CTR'
47	        ? await encryptAesCtr(data, fileKey, iv)
48	        : await encryptAesGcm(data, fileKey, iv);
49	
50	    const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, userPublicKey);
51	
52	    // Defensive copy of fileKey before clearing internal one
53	    const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);
54	    clearBytes(fileKey);
55	
56	    const response: EncryptResponse = {
57	      type: 'success',
58	      id,
59	      ciphertext,
60	      wrappedKey: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),
61	      iv: bytesToHex(iv),
62	      fileKey: fileKeyCopy,
63	      originalSize: data.byteLength,
64	      encryptedSize: ciphertext.byteLength,
65	    };
66	
67	    // Transfer ownership of large buffers (zero-copy)
68	    self.postMessage(response, [
69	      ciphertext.buffer,
70	      fileKeyCopy.buffer,
71	    ] as Transferable[]);
72	  } catch (err) {
73	    const response: EncryptResponse = {
74	      type: 'error',
75	      id,
76	      error: (err as Error).message,
77	    };
78	    self.postMessage(response);
79	  }
80	};
81

830	   * Runs encrypt+pin operations concurrently (max UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY slots),
831	   * collects results, re-reads folder metadata for stale-children mitigation,
832	   * and publishes all successful FilePointers in one atomic update.
833	   *
834	   * Per D-03: New batch method. Per D-01: Fixed concurrency of 3.
835	   * Per D-02: Pipeline-style (encrypt -> pin -> free per file, not buffer-all).
836	   * Per D-05: Re-reads folder metadata before final publish.
837	   * Per D-09: Partial failures publish successes only.
838	   * Per D-10: Single publish after all slots drain.
839	   */
840	  async uploadFiles(
841	    folderIpnsName: string,
842	    files: Array<{ data: Uint8Array; fileName: string; mimeType: string }>,
843	    callbacks?: {
844	      onFileProgress?: (fileName: string, percent: number) => void;
845	      onFileComplete?: (fileName: string) => void;
846	      onFileError?: (fileName: string, error: string) => void;
847	    },
848	    options?: {
849	      encryptFn?: ExternalEncryptFn;
850	      pinFn?: (
851	        ctx: SdkContext,
852	        data: Uint8Array,
853	        onProgress?: ProgressCallback
854	      ) => Promise<{ cid: string; size: number }>;
855	    }
856	  ): Promise<{
857	    successes: Array<{ fileName: string; cid: string }>;
858	    failures: Array<{ fileName: string; error: string }>;
859	  }> {
860	    return this.withOperation('uploadFiles', async () => {
861	      const folder = this.folderTree.get(folderIpnsName);
862	      if (!folder) throw new Error('Folder not loaded');
863	
864	      // Build BYO-IPFS pinFn override (same pattern as uploadFile)
865	      const mode = this.config.pinningConfig?.mode ?? 'cipherbox';
866	      const pinFn =
867	        options?.pinFn ??
868	        (mode === 'cipherbox' || !this.externalProvider
869	          ? undefined
870	          : async (
871	              ctx: SdkContext,
872	              encData: Uint8Array,
873	              prog?: ProgressCallback
874	            ): Promise<{ cid: string; size: number }> => {
875	              const result = await this.pinWithMode(encData, ctx, prog);
876	              return { cid: result.cid, size: result.size };
877	            });
878	
879	      // Create p-limit concurrency pool (D-01)
880	      const limit = pLimit(UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY);
881	
882	      type FileResult = {
883	        fileName: string;
884	        fileId: string;
885	        uploadResult: UploadResult;
886	      };
887	
888	      // Run all files through the pool with Promise.allSettled (D-02, D-10)
889	      const settled = await Promise.allSettled(
890	        files.map((file) =>
891	          limit(async () => {
892	            const fileId = crypto.randomUUID();
893	            const encryptionMode = selectEncryptionMode(file.mimeType, file.data.length);
894	
895	            const uploadResult = await sdkCore.uploadFile({
896	              data: file.data,
897	              fileId,

23	/**
24	 * Hook for handling external file drops (from Finder/Explorer) anywhere in the app.
25	 *
26	 * Uses the SDK's uploadFiles() batch method for new files (D-03: single folder
27	 * publish for the entire batch) with Web Worker encryption (D-07).
28	 * Duplicate files use the old encrypt+upload path for the Replace dialog.
29	 * All IPNS state is managed by the SDK -- no dual-path conflicts.
30	 */
31	export function useDropUpload() {
32	  const isUploading = useUploadStore((s) => {
33	    for (const f of s.files.values()) {
34	      if (f.status === 'encrypting' || f.status === 'uploading') return true;
35	    }
36	    return false;
37	  });
38	
39	  const handleFileDrop = useCallback(async (files: File[], folderId: string): Promise<boolean> => {
40	    // Filter out oversized files
41	    const oversized = files.filter((f) => f.size > MAX_FILE_SIZE);
42	    if (oversized.length > 0) {
43	      logger.error(`[Upload] Files exceed 100MB limit: ${oversized.map((f) => f.name).join(', ')}`);
44	      return false;
45	    }
46	
47	    if (files.length === 0) return false;
48	
49	    // Check quota
50	    const totalSize = files.reduce((sum, f) => sum + f.size, 0);
51	    const quotaStore = useQuotaStore.getState();
52	    if (!quotaStore.canUpload(totalSize)) {
53	      logger.error('[Upload] Not enough storage space for these files');
54	      return false;
55	    }
56	
57	    // Check for duplicates within batch
58	    const batchNames = new Set<string>();
59	    for (const f of files) {
60	      if (batchNames.has(f.name)) {
61	        logger.error(`[Upload] Duplicate file name in selection: ${f.name}`);
62	        return false;
63	      }
64	      batchNames.add(f.name);
65	    }
66	
67	    // Identify which files already exist in the target folder
68	    const folder = useFolderStore.getState().folders[folderId];
69	    const existingByName = new Map<string, string>(); // name -> fileId
70	    const existingFolderNames = new Set<string>();
71	    if (folder) {
72	      for (const child of folder.children) {
73	        if (child.type === 'file') {
74	          existingByName.set(child.name, child.id);
75	        } else if (child.type === 'folder') {
76	          existingFolderNames.add(child.name);
77	        }
78	      }
79	    }

> AGENT

Done. Here's what was fixed:

**Fixed (3 issues):**
1. **Type deduplication** — `EncryptionWorkerService`'s inline encryption result type (defined 4 times) now uses `ExternalEncryptFn` from `@cipherbox/sdk-core` via `ReturnType<>` / `Parameters<>`. Single source of truth.
2. **D-0X task-reference comments removed** — 10 instances of `(D-01)`, `(D-03)`, `(D-07)` etc. stripped from `client.ts` and `useDropUpload.ts`. Replaced with actual WHY context where warranted.
3. **Worker defensive copy comment clarified** — explains security intent (clear key material from Worker memory before transfer), not just "defensive copy".

**Acknowledged but not fixed (design trade-offs):**
- **Upfront file buffer allocation** — all 3 agents flagged this. Fixing requires changing the SDK contract to accept `File` objects instead of `Uint8Array`. Bounded by MAX_FILE_SIZE (100MB) in practice. Worth revisiting if batch sizes grow.
- **Blob `.buffer` in upload.service.ts** — pre-existing code (not Phase 37), violates `apps/web/CLAUDE.md` guidance. Should be fixed separately.

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me provide a comprehensive report.

## CipherBox SDK Upload Integration Tests - Search Results

### Summary

I've completed a thorough search of the CipherBox codebase for SDK integration tests related to upload functionality. Here's what I found:

---

## 1. Integration Tests for Upload Methods

**STATUS: PARTIAL**

### Integration Tests That Exist:

1. **`packages/sdk/src/__tests__/integration.test.ts`** (Live API Integration Tests)
   - Tests **single-file `uploadFile()` only** (line 83)
   - Full lifecycle: upload → download via IPNS → verify content byte-for-byte
   - Tests folder CRUD alongside file operations
   - Bin lifecycle test also uses `uploadFile()` 
   - Requires local API running (`NODE_ENV=test`)
   - Skipped in CI (`describeIf = process.env.CI ? describe.skip : describe`)

2. **`tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/file-operations.test.ts`** (E2E Suite)
   - Tests multiple file sizes: 100B, 50KB, 500KB
   - Round-trip verification with byte-for-byte content checking
   - Subfolder uploads
   - File rename and delete operations
   - Uses `uploadFile()` (singular), not `uploadFiles()` (batch)
   - Runs against full API stack with real vault initialization

### Gap: `uploadFiles()` (batch method) has NO integration tests

The new batch method `uploadFiles()` (line 839 in `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts`) has:
- **Unit tests only** in `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/upload-batch.test.ts` (all mocked)
- **Zero integration/E2E tests** verifying actual server behavior

---

## 2. What Upload Testing Exists vs. Missing

| Method | Unit Tests | Integration Tests | E2E Tests | Notes |
|--------|-----------|------------------|-----------|-------|
| `uploadFile()` (singular) | Yes | Yes (`integration.test.ts`) | Yes (`file-operations.test.ts`) | Full coverage |
| `uploadFiles()` (batch) | Yes | **MISSING** | **MISSING** | Mocks everything |

---

## 3. Unit Tests Coverage for `uploadFiles()`

Location: `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/__tests__/upload-batch.test.ts` (333 lines)

**What's tested (all mocked):**
- Concurrency pool limiting (`UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY=3`)
- Single folder publish call for N files
- Stale-children re-read before publish (D-05 requirement)
- Partial failure handling (D-09 requirement)
- Per-file progress and completion callbacks
- Event emission (`files:batchUploaded`, `folder:updated`)
- Key cleanup in finally block
- onFileError callback

**What's NOT tested (would need integration tests):**
- Actual wiring with the real API
- Real batch encryption pipeline
- Real IPFS pinning behavior
- Real IPNS record publication
- Actual file serialization to the API
- Concurrent request load on a real server
- API error handling (rate limits, timeouts, etc.)
- Network-level retry behavior

---

## 4. Testing Strategy Document

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`

**Key findings:**
- Project uses three test tiers:
  1. **SDK E2E** (`tests/sdk-e2e/`) — 83 tests, ~11s runtime
  2. **Load tests** (`tests/load/`) — 5 performance scenarios

## Web Worker Setup Investigation Report

I've completed a thorough investigation of the encrypt worker setup across all build environments. Here's what I found:

### 1. **Vite Worker Support** ✓ CORRECT

The worker instantiation in `apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts` (lines 29-32) uses the correct Vite syntax:

```typescript
this.worker = new Worker(
  new URL('../workers/encrypt.worker.ts', import.meta.url),
  { type: 'module' },
);
```

This is the exact pattern Vite expects for static analysis and native worker handling. Vite automatically:
- Detects the `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` pattern
- Compiles the worker TypeScript inline
- Outputs it as a separate bundle
- Returns the worker URL at runtime

The vite.config.ts has no special worker configuration needed—Vite handles this natively without explicit plugins.

### 2. **TypeScript Configuration** ⚠️ ISSUE FOUND

**Problem:** The encrypt worker is excluded from the main tsconfig but has NO separate tsconfig configuration:

**Main tsconfig.json** (line 14):
```json
"exclude": ["src/workers/decrypt-sw.ts", "src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts"]
```

**Service Worker has separate config** (`tsconfig.sw.json`):
```json
{
  "lib": ["ES2020", "WebWorker"],
  "include": ["src/workers/decrypt-sw.ts"]
}
```

**Gap:** The encrypt worker is excluded but NOT included in any tsconfig. This means:
- `tsc -b` (build script, line 8 of package.json) won't type-check the worker
- Type checking in IDEs may show false errors inside the worker
- Vite will still compile it (Vite doesn't require tsc to process .ts files), but CI type validation is incomplete

**Recommendation:** Add the encrypt worker to tsconfig.sw.json or create a separate tsconfig.worker.json:

```json
// tsconfig.worker.json
{
  "extends": "../../tsconfig.base.json",
  "compilerOptions": {
    "target": "ES2020",
    "lib": ["ES2020", "WebWorker"],
    "module": "ESNext",
    "noEmit": true,
    "declaration": false
  },
  "include": ["src/workers/*.worker.ts"]
}
```

Then add to package.json build script:
```json
"build": "tsc -b && tsc -p tsconfig.sw.json --noEmit && tsc -p tsconfig.worker.json --noEmit && vite build && node scripts/build-sw.mjs"
```

### 3. **Worker Imports from @cipherbox/crypto** ✓ CORRECT

The worker imports (lines 3-12 of encrypt.worker.ts):
```typescript
import {
  generateFileKey,
  generateIv,
  generateCtrIv,
  encryptAesGcm,
  encryptAesCtr,
  wrapKey,
  clearBytes,
  bytesToHex,
} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
```

All these functions are properly exported from `packages/crypto/src/index.ts` (lines 51-81). **Critical verification:**

- **Web Crypto API only**: All functions use Web Crypto API exclusively:
  - `encryptAesGcm` and `encryptAesCtr` use `crypto.subtle.encrypt`
  - `generateFileKey` uses `crypto.getRandomValues`
  - `wrapKey` uses `eciesjs` (a pure JS ECIES implementation that internally calls Web Crypto)
  - `clearBytes` and `bytesToHex` are pure utility functions

- **No Node.js APIs**: The crypto package declares NO dependencies on Node.js modules (no `fs`, `path`, `crypto` module). Dependencies are all browser-safe:
  - `@libp2p/crypto`, `@libp2p/peer-id` (libp2p is isomorphic)
  - `@noble/ed25519`, `@noble/hashes` (pure JS libraries, browser-safe)
  - `eciesjs` (browser library)
  - `ipns`, `multiformats` (isomorphic)

**Result:** The worker will successfully resolve and execute all imports in a Worker context.

### 4. **Existing Worker Pattern** ✓ PRECEDENT EXISTS

The decrypt service worker (`apps/web/src/workers/decrypt-sw.ts`) shows the project already has working Worker infrastructure:

- It's a Service Worker that does NOT import from `@cipherbox/crypto`
- It implements decryption inline using only Web Crypto API primitives
- It's built via a separate post-build step (`scripts/build-sw.mjs`) that outputs it as an IIFE

**Key difference:** The decrypt-sw.ts is built as IIFE via `vite build --lib` in a post-build step, while the encrypt.worker.ts is built as an ES module via standard Vite build. Both approaches are valid and Vite supports both.

The encrypt worker pattern (ES module, imported with `new Worker(new URL(...))`) is actually cleaner and more modern than the decrypt-sw IIFE pattern.

### 5. **CI Build** ✓ CORRECT

CI workflow (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, line 525):
```bash
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web build
```

The build script runs:
```json
"build": "tsc -b && tsc -p tsconfig.sw.json --noEmit && vite build && node scripts/build-sw.mjs"
```

This will:
1. Type-check main code via `tsc -b`
2. Type-check service worker via `tsc -p tsconfig.sw.json`
3. Build everything via `vite build` (which handles the encrypt.worker.ts)
4. Build the decrypt service worker as a post-build step

✓ The worker will be bundled correctly in CI.

### 6. **Content Security Policy** ✓ NO CSP BLOCKING

Checked:
- **Caddyfile** (docker/Caddyfile): No CSP headers configured
- **index.html** (apps/web/index.html): No CSP meta tag
- **Vite config**: No CSP headers added via middleware

**Result:** No CSP restrictions on `worker-src` or `script-src`. Workers will load and execute correctly.

---

## Summary of Issues & Recommendations

| Issue | Severity | Status | Fix |
|-------|----------|--------|-----|
| Encrypt worker not in any tsconfig | Medium | **Gap** | Add to tsconfig.worker.json and update build script |
| Missing type-checking for worker in CI | Medium | **Gap** | Add worker tsconfig check to `tsc` invocations |
| All imports use Web Crypto API | Low | ✓ Verified | No action needed |
| Vite worker syntax correct | Low | ✓ Verified | No action needed |
| No CSP blocking | Low | ✓ Verified | No action needed |
| Transferable handling correct | Low | ✓ Verified | No action needed (lines 68-72) |

**Primary action:** Update the TypeScript configuration to include type-checking of the encrypt worker alongside the service worker checks.

1	/**
2	 * Domain-specific assertion helpers for SDK E2E tests.
3	 */
4	
5	import { expect } from 'vitest';
6	import type { CipherBoxClient } from '@cipherbox/sdk';
7	import type { FolderChild } from '@cipherbox/core';
8	
9	/**
10	 * Assert that a folder's in-memory children contain an item with the given name.
11	 */
12	export function expectChildNamed(
13	  client: CipherBoxClient,
14	  folderIpnsName: string,
15	  name: string
16	): void {
17	  const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(folderIpnsName);
18	  expect(folder, `Folder ${folderIpnsName} not found in tree`).toBeTruthy();
19	  const match = folder!.children.find((c) => c.name === name);
20	  expect(match, `Expected child named "${name}" in folder`).toBeTruthy();
21	}
22	
23	/**
24	 * Assert that a folder does NOT contain an item with the given name.
25	 */
26	export function expectNoChildNamed(
27	  client: CipherBoxClient,
28	  folderIpnsName: string,
29	  name: string
30	): void {
31	  const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(folderIpnsName);
32	  expect(folder, `Folder ${folderIpnsName} not found in tree`).toBeTruthy();
33	  const match = folder!.children.find((c) => c.name === name);
34	  expect(match, `Expected no child named "${name}" in folder`).toBeUndefined();
35	}
36	
37	/**
38	 * Assert that a folder has exactly N children.
39	 */
40	export function expectChildCount(
41	  client: CipherBoxClient,
42	  folderIpnsName: string,
43	  count: number
44	): void {
45	  const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(folderIpnsName);
46	  expect(folder, `Folder ${folderIpnsName} not found in tree`).toBeTruthy();
47	  expect(folder!.children.length).toBe(count);
48	}
49	
50	/**
51	 * Get a child entry from the folder tree by name.
52	 * Throws if not found.
53	 */
54	export function getChild(
55	  client: CipherBoxClient,
56	  folderIpnsName: string,
57	  name: string
58	): FolderChild {
59	  const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(folderIpnsName);
60	  if (!folder) throw new Error(`Folder ${folderIpnsName} not in tree`);
61	  const match = folder.children.find((c) => c.name === name);
62	  if (!match) throw new Error(`Child "${name}" not found in folder`);
63	  return match;
64	}
65	
66	/**
67	 * Get all children from the folder tree.
68	 */
69	export function getChildren(client: CipherBoxClient, folderIpnsName: string): FolderChild[] {
70	  const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(folderIpnsName);
71	  if (!folder) throw new Error(`Folder ${folderIpnsName} not in tree`);
72	  return folder.children;
73	}
74	
75	/**
76	 * Assert byte-for-byte equality between two Uint8Arrays.
77	 */
78	export function expectBytesEqual(actual: Uint8Array, expected: Uint8Array): void {
79	  expect(actual.length).toBe(expected.length);
80	  expect(Buffer.from(actual).equals(Buffer.from(expected))).toBe(true);
81	}

1	/**
2	 * Test data generators for SDK E2E tests.
3	 * All data is in-memory Uint8Array — no filesystem temp files needed.
4	 */
5	
6	/**
7	 * Generate a Uint8Array of the given size filled with a repeating pattern.
8	 * The pattern is deterministic for a given seed, enabling content verification.
9	 */
10	export function generateBytes(sizeBytes: number, seed = 42): Uint8Array {
11	  const data = new Uint8Array(sizeBytes);
12	  for (let i = 0; i < sizeBytes; i++) {
13	    data[i] = (seed + i * 7 + (i >> 8) * 13) & 0xff;
14	  }
15	  return data;
16	}
17	
18	/** Generate a text content Uint8Array */
19	export function generateTextContent(text: string): Uint8Array {
20	  return new TextEncoder().encode(text);
21	}
22	
23	/** Decode Uint8Array back to text */
24	export function decodeText(data: Uint8Array): string {
25	  return new TextDecoder().decode(data);
26	}
27	
28	/** Common test file sizes */
29	export const FILE_SIZES = {
30	  tiny: 100, // 100 bytes

> AGENT

Here's the summary of what was found and fixed:

### What was missing

| Area | Status | Action taken |
|------|--------|-------------|
| **SDK integration test** | Missing — `uploadFiles()` had zero integration tests | Added 3-file batch test with round-trip content verification to `integration.test.ts` |
| **SDK E2E test suite** | Missing — no `batch-upload.test.ts` existed | Created 5-test suite: basic batch, content round-trip, mixed sizes (100B–500KB), progress callbacks, event emission |
| **Web E2E tests** | Not updated — all upload tests use serial `uploadFile()` | Not changed here — these test via browser UI with real drag-drop, harder to add without a running app. Existing upload E2E tests still cover the single-file path. |
| **Worker tsconfig** | Worker excluded from type-checking in CI | Added `encrypt.worker.ts` to `tsconfig.sw.json` — CI now type-checks it |

### Worker build compatibility — already correct

- Vite handles `new Worker(new URL(..., import.meta.url))` natively — no config needed
- `@cipherbox/crypto` uses **Web Crypto API only** (no Node.js `crypto`) — safe in Worker context
- No CSP headers blocking `worker-src`
- Follows same pattern as existing `decrypt-sw.ts` worker

- Threads 1, 2: Real bug — `params.data.length` becomes 0 after Transferable transfer
- Thread 4: Valid — `addFilePointerToFolder` collision should not abort entire batch

  "old_string": "        // Add FilePointers for all successful uploads\n        for (const success of successes) {\n          const { updatedChildren } = sdkCore.addFilePointerToFolder({\n            children: mergedChildren,\n            fileId: success.fileId,\n            fileName: success.fileName,\n            fileMetaIpnsName: success.uploadResult.fileMetaIpnsName,\n            ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: success.uploadResult.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted,\n          });\n          mergedChildren = updatedChildren;\n        }\n\n        // Single folder publish + batch IPNS publish (concurrent)\n        const ipnsRecords = successes.map((s) => s.uploadResult.ipnsRecord);",
  "new_string": "        // Add FilePointers for all successful uploads (skip collisions gracefully)\n        const registeredSuccesses: FileResult[] = [];\n        for (const success of successes) {\n          try {\n            const { updatedChildren } = sdkCore.addFilePointerToFolder({\n              children: mergedChildren,\n              fileId: success.fileId,\n              fileName: success.fileName,\n              fileMetaIpnsName: success.uploadResult.fileMetaIpnsName,\n              ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: success.uploadResult.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted,\n            });\n            mergedChildren = updatedChildren;\n            registeredSuccesses.push(success);\n          } catch (err) {\n            // Name collision from concurrent upload on another device — treat as failure\n            const errorMsg = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);\n            failures.push({ fileName: success.fileName, error: errorMsg });\n            callbacks?.onFileError?.(success.fileName, errorMsg);\n          }\n        }\n\n        // If all registrations failed after collision handling, skip publish\n        if (registeredSuccesses.length === 0) {\n          this.emitter.emit({\n            type: 'files:batchUploaded',\n            folderId: folderIpnsName,\n            successes: [],\n            failures: failures.map((f) => ({ fileName: f.fileName, error: f.error })),\n          });\n          return { successes: [], failures };\n        }\n\n        // Single folder publish + batch IPNS publish (concurrent)\n        const ipnsRecords = registeredSuccesses.map((s) => s.uploadResult.ipnsRecord);"
}

123	        }
124

127	        for (const file of newFiles) {
128	          const uploadId = uploadIdMap.get(file.name)!;
129	          // Check if cancelled before reading
130	          if (!useUploadStore.getState().files.get(uploadId)) {
131	            continue; // User cancelled this file before it started
132	          }
133	          const data = new Uint8Array(await file.arrayBuffer());
134	          fileEntries.push({
135	            data,
136	            fileName: file.name,
137	            mimeType: file.type || 'application/octet-stream',
138	          });
139	        }
140

142	          // Get encryption Worker's encryptFn for off-main-thread encryption

144	          const encryptFn = encryptionWorker.createEncryptFn();
145

147	            parentFolder.ipnsName,

151	                const uploadId = uploadIdMap.get(fileName);
152	                if (uploadId) {
153	                  useUploadStore.getState().updateFileProgress(uploadId, percent);
154	                }
155	              },
156	              onFileComplete: (fileName) => {
157	                const uploadId = uploadIdMap.get(fileName);
158	                if (uploadId) {
159	                  useUploadStore.getState().setFileStatus(uploadId, 'complete');
160	                }
161	              },
162	              onFileError: (fileName, error) => {
163	                const uploadId = uploadIdMap.get(fileName);
164	                if (uploadId) {
165	                  useUploadStore.getState().setFileStatus(uploadId, 'error', error);
166	                }
167	              },
168	            },
169	            { encryptFn }
170	          );
171	
172	          // Failures already surfaced via onFileError callback (sets upload store to 'error').
173	          if (result.failures.length > 0) {
174	            logger.warn(
175	              `[Upload] Batch upload partial failure: ${result.failures.length} file(s) failed`,
176	              result.failures
177	            );
178	          }
179	        }
180	      }
181	
182	      // Handle duplicate files: encrypt + upload to IPFS, then surface as pending replacements
183	      // (These don't get registered in the folder -- the user decides via the Replace dialog)

Good. The CONTEXT.md has 3 deferred ideas, but none of the PR review deferrals are there. The deferred items from the PR reviews are:

1. **Secondary pin warning for batch uploads** — BYO-IPFS `pinFn` wrapper discards `secondaryWarning`, so `pin:secondaryFailed` events don't emit for batch uploads
2. **AbortSignal/cancel support for in-flight batch uploads** — No way to cancel once `uploadFiles()` is invoked; requires SDK contract change
3. **Lazy file reading within concurrency pool** — Currently reads all file buffers upfront; should accept `File` objects and read lazily to bound memory

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "new_string": "- **Accumulated retry batching** — If many files fail and are retried, batch the retries into a single folder publish instead of N individual publishes. Implement if failure rates warrant it.\n- **Batch upload secondary pin warning events** — BYO-IPFS `pinFn` wrapper in `uploadFiles()` discards `secondaryWarning` from `pinWithMode()`, so `pin:secondaryFailed` events are never emitted for batch uploads in dual-pin mode. Needs per-file warning capture and a batch equivalent event. (PR #416 review — Copilot thread 3)\n- **AbortSignal support for in-flight batch uploads** — Once `client.uploadFiles()` is invoked, there is no way to cancel in-flight or not-yet-started uploads from the UI. Requires adding an `AbortSignal` parameter to `uploadFiles()` and propagating it through p-limit and `sdkCore.uploadFile()`. The pre-upload cancel check (before `arrayBuffer()`) is the only cancellation point today. (PR #416 review — Copilot thread 6, CodeRabbit thread 9)\n- **Lazy file reading within concurrency pool** — `useDropUpload` reads all file buffers into `Uint8Array` upfront before calling `uploadFiles()`. For large batches this allocates all memory simultaneously instead of pipelining reads through the p-limit pool. Fix requires changing the SDK contract to accept `File` objects (or a read callback) instead of `Uint8Array`. Bounded today by MAX_FILE_SIZE=100MB. (PR #416 review — CodeRabbit thread 8)\n\n### Reviewed Todos (not folded)"
}

Thread 1: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53wOpz
Author: coderabbitai
File: apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts:70
CommentID: 3011302162
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Clear the generated file key on every exit path.**

If `encryptAes*()`, `wrapKey()`, or even `postMessage()` throws after `generateFileKey()`, the plaintext AES key never hits `clearBytes()` and survives in this long-lived worker until GC. Mirror the SDK-core cleanup pattern here with a `finally`.

 self.onmes

Thread 2: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53wOp_
Author: coderabbitai
File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:909
CommentID: 3011302179
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Defer `onFileComplete` until the batch commit succeeds.**

This fires as soon as `sdkCore.uploadFile()` resolves, but `addFilePointerToFolder()` and the final folder publish can still fail afterwards. In the web caller, that marks a row `complete` before the batch is actually committed, so a later collision or publish error can leave a false success in the UI. E

---
Thread 3: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53wPCW
Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:908
CommentID: 3011304333
Body: `uploadFiles()` invokes `onFileComplete` immediately after `sdkCore.uploadFile` resolves, but before name-collision handling (`addFilePointerToFolder`) and before the single folder publish succeeds. In failure/collision cases this can temporarily mark a file as complete and trigger auto-removal in `upload.store.ts`, then later flip to error (or the overall publish can throw), leaving the UI incons

Body: In the `encryptFn` path, `uploadFile()` keeps and returns `extResult.fileKey` but the `finally` block only clears `fileKeyInternal`. If a later step throws (pinning/IPFS upload/metadata creation), the caller never receives the `UploadResult` and therefore cannot clear the externally-produced `fileKey`, leaving key material in memory longer than intended. Consider defensively clearing the external 

---
Thread 5: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53wPDh

Body: `encrypt-worker.service.ts` imports `EncryptRequest/EncryptResponse` from `encrypt.worker.ts`, but `encrypt.worker.ts` is also excluded from `apps/web/tsconfig.json` and uses `/// <reference lib="webworker" />`. 107	  return withPerf('upload:full', async () => {
108	    const mode = normalizeEncryptionMode(params.encryptionMode);
109	
110	    // Capture original size before any Transferable detachment (encryptFn may
111	    // transfer params.data.buffer to a Worker, making params.data.length = 0)
112	    const originalSize = params.data.length;
113	
114	    // Internal file key -- only generated when encryptFn is NOT provided.
115	    // When encryptFn is provided, the caller owns the returned fileKey memory.
116	    let fileKeyInternal: Uint8Array | null = null;
117	
118	    try {
119	      let ciphertext: Uint8Array;
120	      let wrappedKeyHex: string;
121	      let ivHex: string;
122	      let fileKeyForResult: Uint8Array;
123	
124	      if (params.encryptFn) {
125	        // External encryption path (e.g., Web Worker)
126	        const extResult = await params.encryptFn({
127	          data: params.data,
128	          userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
129	          encryptionMode: mode,
130	        });
131	        ciphertext = extResult.ciphertext;
132	        wrappedKeyHex = extResult.wrappedKey;
133	        ivHex = extResult.iv;
134	        fileKeyForResult = extResult.fileKey;
135	      } else {
136	        // Internal encryption path (original behavior)
137	        // 1. Generate unique file key and IV (CTR uses 16-byte nonce+counter, GCM uses 12-byte random)
138	        fileKeyInternal = generateFileKey();
139	        const iv = mode === 'CTR' ? generateCtrIv() : generateIv();
140	
141	        // 2. Encrypt file content
142	        ciphertext =
143	          mode === 'CTR'
144	            ? await encryptAesCtr(params.data, fileKeyInternal, iv)
145	            : await encryptAesGcm(params.data, fileKeyInternal, iv);
146	
147	        // 3. Wrap file key with user's public key (ECIES)
148	        const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKeyInternal, params.userPublicKey);
149	        wrappedKeyHex = bytesToHex(wrappedKey);
150	        ivHex = bytesToHex(iv);
151	
152	        // Return a defensive copy of the file key for re-wrapping.
153	        // The caller is responsible for clearing it after use.
154	        fileKeyForResult = new Uint8Array(fileKeyInternal);
155	      }
156	
157	      // 4. Upload encrypted content to IPFS (or BYO node via pinFn override)
158	      const pinResult = params.pinFn
159	        ? await withPerf('ipfs:upload:byo', () =>
160	            params.pinFn!(params.ctx, ciphertext, params.onProgress)
161	          )
162	        : await addToIpfs(params.ctx, ciphertext, params.onProgress);
163	      const { cid, size: encryptedSize } = pinResult;
164	
165	      // 5. Create per-file IPNS metadata record
166	      const fileMetaResult = await createFileMetadata({
167	        fileId: params.fileId,
168	        cid,
169	        fileKeyEncrypted: wrappedKeyHex,
170	        fileIv: ivHex,
171	        size: originalSize,
172	        mimeType: params.mimeType,
173	        folderKey: params.folderKey,
174	        userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
175	        ctx: params.ctx,
176	        teeKeys: params.teeKeys,
177	        encryptionMode: mode,
178	      });
179	
180	      return {
181	        cid,
182	        encryptedSize,
183	        fileMetaIpnsName: fileMetaResult.fileMetaIpnsName,
184	        ipnsRecord: fileMetaResult.ipnsRecord,
185	        ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: fileMetaResult.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted,
186	        fileKey: fileKeyForResult,
187	      };
188	    } finally {
189	      // 6. Clear the internal copy of the key from memory (only if internally generated)
190	      if (fileKeyInternal) {
191	        clearBytes(fileKeyInternal);
192	      }
193	    }
194	  });
195	}

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "self.onmessage = async (event: MessageEvent<EncryptRequest>) => {\n  const { id, data, userPublicKey, encryptionMode } = event.data;\n  try {\n    const fileKey = generateFileKey();\n    const iv = encryptionMode === 'CTR' ? generateCtrIv() : generateIv();\n\n    const ciphertext =\n      encryptionMode === 'CTR'\n        ? await encryptAesCtr(data, fileKey, iv)\n        : await encryptAesGcm(data, fileKey, iv);\n\n    const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, userPublicKey);\n\n    // Copy fileKey before clearing — clearBytes must happen before postMessage\n    // so the key material doesn't linger in Worker memory after transfer\n    const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);\n    clearBytes(fileKey);\n\n    const response: EncryptResponse = {\n      type: 'success',\n      id,\n      ciphertext,\n      wrappedKey: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),\n      iv: bytesToHex(iv),\n      fileKey: fileKeyCopy,\n      originalSize: data.byteLength,\n      encryptedSize: ciphertext.byteLength,\n    };\n\n    // Transfer ownership of large buffers (zero-copy)\n    self.postMessage(response, [ciphertext.buffer, fileKeyCopy.buffer] as Transferable[]);\n  } catch (err) {\n    const response: EncryptResponse = {\n      type: 'error',\n      id,\n      error: (err as Error).message,\n    };\n    self.postMessage(response);\n  }\n};",
  "new_string": "self.onmessage = async (event: MessageEvent<EncryptRequest>) => {\n  const { id, data, userPublicKey, encryptionMode } = event.data;\n  let fileKey: Uint8Array | null = null;\n  try {\n    fileKey = generateFileKey();\n    const iv = encryptionMode === 'CTR' ? generateCtrIv() : generateIv();\n\n    const ciphertext =\n      encryptionMode === 'CTR'\n        ? await encryptAesCtr(data, fileKey, iv)\n        : await encryptAesGcm(data, fileKey, iv);\n\n    const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, userPublicKey);\n\n    // Copy fileKey before clearing — clearBytes must happen before postMessage\n    // so the key material doesn't linger in Worker memory after transfer\n    const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);\n    clearBytes(fileKey);\n    fileKey = null; // Prevent double-clear in finally\n\n    const response: EncryptResponse = {\n      type: 'success',\n      id,\n      ciphertext,\n      wrappedKey: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),\n      iv: bytesToHex(iv),\n      fileKey: fileKeyCopy,\n      originalSize: data.byteLength,\n      encryptedSize: ciphertext.byteLength,\n    };\n\n    // Transfer ownership of large buffers (zero-copy)\n    self.postMessage(response, [ciphertext.buffer, fileKeyCopy.buffer] as Transferable[]);\n  } catch (err) {\n    const response: EncryptResponse = {\n      type: 'error',\n      id,\n      error: (err as Error).message,\n    };\n    self.postMessage(response);\n  } finally {\n    // Clear key material on any exit path (encrypt/wrap/postMessage failure)\n    if (fileKey) {\n      clearBytes(fileKey);\n    }\n  }\n};"
}

134:            const data = new Uint8Array(await file.arrayBuffer());
```

> AGENT

The data comes from `new Uint8Array(await file.arrayBuffer())` — this creates a fresh `Uint8Array` wrapping the entire `ArrayBuffer`, so `byteOffset` is always 0 and `byteLength === buffer.byteLength`. It's never a subview in the current call path.

However, `encrypt()` is a public method on `EncryptionWorkerService`, and `ExternalEncryptFn` accepts any `Uint8Array` — a caller could pass a subview (e.g., `data.subarray(0, 100)`). In that case, transferring `.buffer` would detach the entire underlying ArrayBuffer, which is a legitimate defensive concern.

Worth fixing — it's a one-line guard and prevents a subtle bug if the calling code ever changes.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01FWBmnv7jEx99jkqgobnFfJ
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/services/encrypt-worker.service.ts",
  "old_string": "      // Transfer the data buffer to worker (zero-copy, original becomes empty)\n      worker.postMessage(message, [params.data.buffer] as Transferable[]);",
  "new_string": "      // Transfer the data buffer to worker (zero-copy, original becomes empty).\n      // If data is a subview, copy first — transferring .buffer would detach\n      // the entire underlying ArrayBuffer beyond the intended range.\n      const isFullBuffer =\n        params.data.byteOffset === 0 &&\n        params.data.byteLength === params.data.buffer.byteLength;\n      const transferData = isFullBuffer ? params.data : params.data.slice();\n      message.data = transferData;\n      worker.postMessage(message, [transferData.buffer] as Transferable[]);"
}
```

Fixed. The current call path (`new Uint8Array(await file.arrayBuffer())`) always creates a full-buffer view, so the guard is a no-op today. But since `encrypt()` is a public method accepting any `Uint8Array`, the defensive check prevents a subtle bug if a subview is ever passed — it copies to a new buffer instead of detaching the entire underlying `ArrayBuffer`.

```

> AGENT

It uses bare `crypto.subtle` — no import, no polyfill. This works because:

1. **Node.js 18+** exposes `crypto` as a global (the Web Crypto API is built into Node.js since v15, and became a stable global in v19+)
2. **Vitest** runs in Node.js, which has `globalThis.crypto.subtle` natively

So there's no actual hard dependency on *browser* Web Crypto — it's the Web Crypto *API*, which Node.js implements identically. The Worker also has it (`self.crypto.subtle`). All three environments (browser main thread, Web Worker, Node.js test runner) provide the same `crypto.subtle` global.

> TOOL

```

```

> AGENT

Confirmed. `crypto.subtle` is a global in Node.js — no polyfill or browser emulation needed. The `@cipherbox/crypto` package is isomorphic by design: it only uses the Web Crypto API standard (which browser, Worker, and Node.js all implement), not any browser-specific APIs like `window` or DOM.

10	export class UploadZonePage {
11	  constructor(private readonly page: Page) {}
12	
13	  /**
14	   * Get the upload zone dropzone area.
15	   */
16	  dropzone(): Locator {
17	    return this.page.locator('.upload-zone');
18	  }
19	
20	  /**
21	   * Get the hidden file input element (used by react-dropzone).
22	   * Checks both the upload zone and the empty state dropzone.
23	   * Uses first() as there may be multiple upload zones on the page.
24	   */
25	  private fileInput(): Locator {
26	    return this.page
27	      .locator('.upload-zone input[type="file"], .empty-state input[type="file"]')
28	      .first();
29	  }
30	
31	  /**
32	   * Get the upload zone error message if displayed.
33	   */
34	  errorMessage(): Locator {
35	    return this.page.locator('.upload-zone-error');
36	  }
37	
38	  /**
39	   * Upload a single file via the file input.
40	   * This is the most reliable method for Playwright tests.
41	   *
42	   * @param filePath - Absolute or relative path to the file
43	   */
44	  async uploadFile(filePath: string): Promise<void> {
45	    // Clear previous selection so re-uploading the same file path triggers change
46	    await this.fileInput().evaluate((el: HTMLInputElement) => {
47	      el.value = '';
48	    });
49	    await this.fileInput().setInputFiles(filePath);
50	  }
51	
52	  /**
53	   * Upload multiple files via the file input.
54	   *
55	   * @param filePaths - Array of file paths
56	   */
57	  async uploadFiles(filePaths: string[]): Promise<void> {
58	    // Clear previous selection so re-uploading the same file paths triggers change
59	    await this.fileInput().evaluate((el: HTMLInputElement) => {
60	      el.value = '';
61	    });
62	    await this.fileInput().setInputFiles(filePaths);
63	  }
64	
65	  /**
