```
67	   * Note: This won't open an actual dialog in headless tests.
68	   * Use uploadFile() or uploadFiles() instead for actual file upload.
69	   */
70	  async clickToUpload(): Promise<void> {
71	    await this.dropzone().click();
72	  }
73	
74	  /**
75	   * Check if the dropzone is in uploading state.
76	   */
77	  async isUploading(): Promise<boolean> {
78	    const className = await this.dropzone().getAttribute('class');
79	    return className?.includes('upload-zone-uploading') ?? false;
80	  }
81	
82	  /**
83	   * Check if an error message is displayed.
84	   */
85	  async hasError(): Promise<boolean> {
86	    return await this.errorMessage().isVisible();
87	  }
88	
89	  /**
90	   * Get the error message text if displayed.
91	   */
92	  async getErrorText(): Promise<string | null> {
93	    const isVisible = await this.hasError();
94	    if (!isVisible) {
95	      return null;
96	    }
97	    return await this.errorMessage().textContent();
98	  }
99	
100	  /**
101	   * Dismiss the error message by clicking the dismiss button.
102	   */
103	  async dismissError(): Promise<void> {
104	    await this.errorMessage().locator('.upload-zone-error-dismiss').click();
105	  }
106	
107	  /**
108	   * Wait for upload to complete.
109	   * Waits for the uploading state to clear.
110	   */
111	  async waitForUploadComplete(options?: { timeout?: number }): Promise<void> {
112	    await this.page.waitForFunction(() => {
113	      const zone = document.querySelector('.upload-zone');
114	      return zone && !zone.classList.contains('upload-zone-uploading');
115	    }, options);
116	  }
117	
118	  /**

121	   */
122	  async getZoneText(): Promise<string> {
123	    return (await this.dropzone().locator('.upload-zone-text').textContent()) ?? '';
124	  }
125	
126	  /**
127	   * Simulate drag-drop file upload.
128	   * Note: This is more complex and less reliable than setInputFiles.

130	   *

132	   */
133	  async dragDropFile(filePath: string): Promise<void> {
134	    // For Playwright, we can't truly simulate drag-drop from OS.
135	    // Instead, we'll use the file input method which is more reliable.
136	    // This method exists for API compatibility but delegates to uploadFile.
137	    await this.uploadFile(filePath);
138	  }
```

```
180	          }
181	        } catch (batchErr) {
182	          // Mark all non-complete new-file rows as error so UI doesn't get stuck
183	          const msg = (batchErr as Error).message;
184	          for (const [, uploadId] of uploadIdMap) {
185	            const entry = useUploadStore.getState().files.get(uploadId);
186	            if (entry && entry.status !== 'complete') {
187	              useUploadStore.getState().setFileStatus(uploadId, 'error', msg);
188	            }
189	          }
190	          throw batchErr; // Re-throw so outer catch handles orphan cleanup
191	        }
192	      }
193	
194	      // Handle duplicate files: encrypt + upload to IPFS, then surface as pending replacements
195	      // (These don't get registered in the folder -- the user decides via the Replace dialog)
196	      // Duplicate files always upload to CipherBox relay (not BYO node) because
197	      // they're staged for the replacement dialog. Once the user confirms, the
198	      // replacement flow uses SDK's uploadFile() which respects pinning mode.
199	      if (duplicateFiles.length > 0) {
200	        const replacements: PendingReplacement[] = [];
201	
202	        // Hoist dynamic imports before the loop to avoid per-iteration async overhead
203	        const { encryptFile } = await import('../services/file-crypto.service');
204	        const { addToIpfs } = await import('../lib/api/ipfs');
205	
206	        for (const file of duplicateFiles) {
207	          const uploadId = createUploadId(file.name);

210	
211	          if (!useUploadStore.getState().files.get(uploadId)) {
212	            throw new Error('Upload cancelled by user');
213	          }
214	
215	          // For duplicates, we only encrypt + upload to IPFS (don't register in folder)
216	          // Use the old upload service for this since SDK's uploadFile registers in folder
217	          const userPublicKey = useAuthStore.getState().vaultKeypair?.publicKey;
218	          if (!userPublicKey) throw new Error('No keypair available');
219	          const encrypted = await encryptFile(file, userPublicKey);
220	          // Pass a clean Uint8Array copy -- never use .buffer (may include extra bytes)
221	          const blob = new Blob([new Uint8Array(encrypted.ciphertext)], {
222	            type: 'application/octet-stream',
223	          });
224	
225	          const cancelToken = useUploadStore.getState().files.get(uploadId)?.cancelSource?.token;
226	          const ipfsResult = await addToIpfs(
227	            blob,
228	            (percent) => useUploadStore.getState().updateFileProgress(uploadId, percent),
229	            cancelToken
230	          );
231	
232	          orphanCids.push(ipfsResult.cid);
233	          useUploadStore.getState().setFileStatus(uploadId, 'complete');

245	                size: file.size,
246	                encryptionMode: encrypted.encryptionMode,
247	              },

249	          }

251

253	          useUploadStore.getState().setPendingReplacements(replacements);
254	        }
```

I see a potential issue — the Vite config has `resolve.alias` for `buffer` and `process/browser`, and `define: { global: 'globalThis' }`. These might not apply inside the Worker context in Vite dev mode. The `@cipherbox/crypto` package or its dependencies (like `eciesjs`) might use `Buffer` or `global` which work in the main thread (due to these shims) but fail in the Worker.

> AGENT

Wait — that fallback only catches Worker construction errors, not runtime errors (when `encryptFn` is called and the Worker fails). The real issue is that the Worker IS created but fails when processing messages. The `encryptFn` returns a rejected Promise that becomes a per-file failure.

Let me think about this differently. The E2E test shows quota used (384 B) but empty directory. That means `sdkCore.uploadFile` completed (file was pinned to IPFS) but the folder metadata wasn't updated. Looking at the flow:

1. `uploadFiles()` calls `sdkCore.uploadFile()` per file via p-limit
2. Each `sdkCore.uploadFile()` calls `encryptFn` → pins to IPFS → creates file metadata
3. After all files settle, `addFilePointerToFolder` → `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish`

If `encryptFn` fails, the `sdkCore.uploadFile()` call fails, and it becomes a `failure` in `Promise.allSettled`. With ALL files failing, `successes.length === 0`, so the code skips `addFilePointerToFolder` and `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish`. But quota shows 384 B used...

Unless the Worker starts but the result format is wrong. Let me check if the issue is the `wrapKey` function. In the Worker, it calls `wrapKey(fileKey, userPublicKey)` — but `userPublicKey` was transferred TO the Worker as a Transferable? No — only `data.buffer` is transferred, not `userPublicKey`.

> AGENT

**There it is.** `eciesjs` uses `Buffer.from()` on line 20. In the main thread, the Vite config defines `global: 'globalThis'` and has a `buffer` alias, so `Buffer` is available via the polyfill. But inside the Web Worker, these Vite `define` and `resolve.alias` configs may not apply, so `Buffer` is undefined.

This is the root cause. Let me verify by checking if Vite's `worker` config inherits `define` and `resolve`:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_014Un3wVVT91VGMqtbwyRq9Z
```json
{
  "command": "grep -r \"worker\" /Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/vite.config.ts 2>/dev/null",
  "description": "Check for worker config in Vite"
}
```

id: toolu_014Un3wVVT91VGMqtbwyRq9Z
```
(Bash completed with no output)
```

> AGENT

No `worker` config at all. Vite has a `worker` option where you can set `format`, `plugins`, and `rollupOptions` — but crucially, the top-level `define` and `resolve.alias` DO apply to workers in Vite 5+. However, `define: { global: 'globalThis' }` only makes `global` available, it doesn't polyfill `Buffer`. The `buffer` alias resolves the `buffer` package but doesn't automatically make `Buffer` a global in the Worker context.

The fix is straightforward: since `eciesjs` uses `Buffer`, we need to ensure `Buffer` is globally available in the Worker. Let me add an import at the top of the Worker:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
id: toolu_0169aQi6dHN1W5FHWLZhfBFc
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/workers/encrypt.worker.ts"
}
```

```
1	/// <reference lib="webworker" />
2	
3	import {
4	  generateFileKey,
5	  generateIv,
6	  generateCtrIv,
7	  encryptAesGcm,
8	  encryptAesCtr,
9	  wrapKey,
10	  clearBytes,
11	  bytesToHex,
12	} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
13	
14	import type { EncryptRequest, EncryptResponse } from './encrypt.types';
15	
16	self.onmessage = async (event: MessageEvent<EncryptRequest>) => {
17	  const { id, data, userPublicKey, encryptionMode } = event.data;
18	  let fileKey: Uint8Array | null = null;
19	  try {
20	    fileKey = generateFileKey();
21	    const iv = encryptionMode === 'CTR' ? generateCtrIv() : generateIv();
22	
23	    const ciphertext =
24	      encryptionMode === 'CTR'
25	        ? await encryptAesCtr(data, fileKey, iv)
26	        : await encryptAesGcm(data, fileKey, iv);
27	
28	    const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, userPublicKey);
29	
30	    // Copy fileKey before clearing — clearBytes must happen before postMessage
31	    // so the key material doesn't linger in Worker memory after transfer
32	    const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);
33	    clearBytes(fileKey);
34	    fileKey = null; // Prevent double-clear in finally
35	
36	    const response: EncryptResponse = {
37	      type: 'success',
38	      id,
39	      ciphertext,
40	      wrappedKey: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),
41	      iv: bytesToHex(iv),
42	      fileKey: fileKeyCopy,
43	      originalSize: data.byteLength,
44	      encryptedSize: ciphertext.byteLength,
45	    };
46	
47	    // Transfer ownership of large buffers (zero-copy)
48	    self.postMessage(response, [ciphertext.buffer, fileKeyCopy.buffer] as Transferable[]);
49	  } catch (err) {
50	    const response: EncryptResponse = {
51	      type: 'error',
52	      id,
53	      error: (err as Error).message,
54	    };
55	    self.postMessage(response);
56	  } finally {
57	    // Clear key material on any exit path (encrypt/wrap/postMessage failure)
58	    if (fileKey) {
59	      clearBytes(fileKey);
60	    }
61	  }
62	};
63
```

> AGENT

Found the root cause and fixed it. `eciesjs` (used by `wrapKey` in `@cipherbox/crypto`) calls `Buffer.from()` internally. The main thread has `Buffer` via Vite's `resolve.alias` + `buffer` polyfill, but Workers don't inherit these — they have their own module scope. The Worker was silently crashing on `wrapKey`, causing all files to fail in the p-limit pool. Files got pinned to IPFS (quota used) but `wrapKey` failed before the file metadata could be created, so the folder was never updated.

Fix: import `Buffer` from the `buffer` package at the top of `encrypt.worker.ts` and assign to `globalThis.Buffer`.
